Annually, we perform a qualitative analysis to determine if it is more likely than not that the fair value has d value. This analysis considers various qualitative factors. Due to the nature of our business, all of our goodwill is associated with on perform our annual impairment testing during the fourth quarter. Based on the results of our analysis, no impairment loss was recog 2019.

At December 31, 2021 and 2020, we had an aggregate carrying amount of goodwill acquired of $21.2 million, which has been reduc impairment charges of $8.5 million. Accordingly, our goodwill balance at December 31, 2021 and 2020 was $12.7 million.

## Health Insurance Costs

We provide group health insurance coverage to our WSEEs in our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions through a national network of car UnitedHealthcare ('United'), UnitedHealthcare of California, Kaiser Permanente, Blue Shield of California, HMSA BlueCross BlueS all of which provide fully insured policies or service contracts.

Approximately 87% of our costs related to health insurance coverage are provided under our policy with United. While the policy wit insured plan, as a result of certain contractual terms, we have accounted for this plan since its inception using a partially self-funded model. Effective January 1, 2020, under the amended agreement with United, we no longer have financial responsibilities for partici that exceed $1 million. Accordingly, we record the cost of the United plan, including an estimate of the incurred claims, taxes and ad (collectively the 'Plan Costs') as benefits expense, which is a component of direct costs, in our Consolidated Statements of Operati incurred claims are based upon: (1) the level of claims processed during each quarter; (2) estimated completion rates based upon r patterns under the plan; and (3) the number of participants in the plan, including both active and COBRA enrollees. Each reporting estimated ultimate costs resulting from claim trends, plan design and migration, participant demographics and other factors are inco costs, which requires a significant level of judgment.

Additionally, since the plan's inception, under the terms of the contract, United establishes cash funding rates 90 days in advance of reporting quarter. If the Plan Costs for a reporting quarter are greater than the premiums paid and owed to United, a deficit in the pl a liability for the excess costs would be accrued in our Consolidated Balance Sheets. On the other hand, if the Plan Costs for the re than the premiums paid and owed to United, a surplus in the plan would be incurred and we would record an asset for the excess pr in our Consolidated Balance Sheets. The terms of the arrangement require us to maintain an accumulated cash surplus in the plan reported as long-term prepaid insurance. In addition, United requires a deposit equal to approximately one day of claims funding act million as of December 31, 2021, and is reported as a long-term asset. As of December 31, 2021, Plan Costs were more than the n owed to United by $22.0 million. As this amount is less than the agreed-upon $9.0 million surplus maintenance level, the $31.0 milli in accrued health insurance costs, a current liability, in our Consolidated Balance Sheets. The premiums, including the additional qu to United at December 31, 2021, were $12.6 million, which is also included in accrued health insurance costs, a current liability in o Sheets. Our benefits costs incurred included an increase of $4.9 million in 2021, a reduction of $0.2 million in 2020 and an increase for changes in estimated run-off related to prior periods.

## Workers' Compensation Costs

Our workers' compensation coverage for our WSEEs in our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions has been provided through an arrange Group of Insurance Companies or its predecessors (the 'Chubb Program') since 2007. The Chubb Program is fully insured in that responsibility to pay all claims incurred under the policy regardless of whether we satisfy our responsibilities. Under the Chubb Prog on or before September 30, 2019, we have financial responsibility to Chubb for the first $1 million layer of claims per occurrence an million, up to a maximum aggregate amount of $6 million per policy year for claims that exceed $1 million. Chubb bears the financial claims in excess of these levels. Effective for claims incurred on or after October 1, 2019, we have financial responsibility to Chubb f layer of claims per occurrence and, for claims over $1.5 million, up to a maximum aggregate amount of $6 million per policy year for million.

Because we bear the financial responsibility for claims up to the levels noted above, such claims, which are the primary component compensation costs, are recorded in the period incurred. Workers' compensation insurance includes ongoing health care and inde claims are paid over numerous years following the date of injury. Accordingly, the accrual of related incurred costs in each reporting estimates, which take into account the ongoing development of claims and therefore requires a significant level of judgment.

We utilize a third-party actuary to estimate our loss development rate, which is primarily based upon the nature of WSEEs' job resp WSEEs, the historical frequency and severity of workers' compensation claims, and an estimate of future cost trends. Each reportin actuarial assumptions resulting from changes in actual claims experience and other trends are incorporated into our workers' comp estimates. During the years ended December 31, 2021, 2020 and 2019, we reduced accrued workers' compensation costs by $41. and $31.7 million, respectively, for changes in estimated losses related to prior reporting periods. Workers' compensation cost estim present value at a rate based upon the U.S. Treasury rates that correspond with the weighted average estimated claim payout perio rate was 0.6% in both 2021 and 2020) and are accreted over the estimated claim payment period and included as a component of d Consolidated Statements of Operations.

## Stock-Based Compensation

At December 31, 2021, we have one stock-based employee compensation plan under which we may issue awards. We account for recognition and measurement principles of ASC 718, Compensation - Stock Compensation , which requires all share-based payme including grants of employee stock options, to be recognized in the income statement based on their fair values.

We generally make annual grants of unrestricted stock under our stock-based incentive compensation plan to our non-employee dir management but changed to restricted stock units for employees in 2020. Restricted stock and restricted stock unit grants to officer generally vest over a period of three years from the date of grant. Shares of restricted stock and restricted stock units are valued ba date of grant and the associated expense, net of estimated forfeitures, is recognized over the requisite service period. Stock grants i directors are 100% vested on the grant date.

During 2020, we also granted performance-based restricted stock units to officers and other management, which vest or expire bas various performance metrics. Performance-based restricted stock units have a vesting schedule of one year. The fair value of each restricted stock unit is the market price of our common stock on the date of grant. The compensation expense for such awards is re line basis over the requisite service period. Over the performance period, the number of shares expected to be issued is adjusted u based on the probability of achievement of the performance target.

Our Insperity Long-Term Incentive Program (the 'LTIP') provides for performance based long-term compensation awards in the for certain employees based on the achievement of pre-established performance goals. Each performance unit represents the right to r share at a future date based on our performance against certain targets. Performance units have a vesting schedule of three years. grant to employees was considered a market-based performance award that cliff vests at the end of three years assuming continue achievement of market-based performance goals. The fair value of each performance unit is the market price of our common stock fair value of each market-based performance unit was determined through use of the Monte Carlo simulation method. The compens awards is recognized on a straight line basis over the vesting term. Over the performance period the number of shares expected to upward or downward based on the probability of achievement of the performance target.

## Company-Sponsored 401(k) Retirement Plans

Under our 401(k) retirement plan for corporate employees (the 'Corporate Plan'), we matched 100% of eligible corporate employee from 3% to 6% of the employees' eligible compensation in 2021 and up to 6% of the employees' eligible compensation in 2020 and contributions under the Corporate Plan are immediately vested. During 2021, 2020 and 2019, we made matching contributions on b employees to the Corporate Plan of $8.2 million, $12.6 million and $11.4 million, respectively, and is included in salaries, wages and Consolidated Statements of Operations.

Under our separate 401(k) retirement plan for WSEEs (the 'Worksite Employee Plan'), the match percentage for WSEEs ranges from 0% to 6%, as determined by each client company. Matching contributions under the Worksite Employee Plan are imme 2021, 2020 and 2019, we made matching contributions on behalf of WSEEs to the Worksite Employee Plan of $244.1 million, $199. million, respectively.

## Long-term Debt (Note)

## Debt Disclosure [Abstract] Long-term Debt

## 6. Long-Term Debt

We have a $500 million revolving credit facility (the 'Facility') which is available for working capital and general corporate purposes, stock repurchases and issuances of letters of credit. Borrowings may be increased to $550 million based on the terms and subject t in the agreement relating to the Facility (as amended, the 'Credit Agreement'). Our obligations under the Facility are secured by 65 captive insurance subsidiary and are guaranteed by all of our domestic subsidiaries other than certain excluded subsidiaries. In add 2021, we had an outstanding $1.0 million letter of credit issued under the Facility. As of December 31, 2021, our outstanding balanc $369.4 million and is due when the Facility matures on September 13, 2024.

Borrowings under the Facility bear interest at an annual rate equal to an alternate base rate or LIBOR, at our option, plus an applica on our leverage ratio, the applicable margin varies (1) in the case of LIBOR loans, from 1.50% to 2.25% and (2) in the case of altern from 0.00% to 0.50%. The alternate base rate is the highest of (1) the prime rate most recently published in The Wall Street Journal rate plus 0.50% and (3) the 30-day LIBOR rate plus 2.00%. We also pay an unused commitment fee on the average daily unused p rate of 0.25%. The average interest rate during 2021 was 1.9%. Interest expense and unused commitment fees are recorded in oth Following early opt-in by us or our lenders or such other time as LIBOR rates have permanently or indefinitely ceased to be publish supervisory authority of LIBOR, then LIBOR will be replaced by a rate per annum equal to the secured overnight financing rate publi Reserve Bank of New York or such other benchmark as determined in accordance with the Credit Agreement.

The Facility contains both affirmative and negative covenants that we believe are customary for arrangements of this nature. Coven limited to, limitations on our ability to incur additional indebtedness, sell material assets, retire, redeem or otherwise reacquire our c capital stock or assets of another business, make investments and pay dividends. In addition, the Credit Agreement requires us to comply with financial covenants li funded debt, minimum interest coverage ratio and maximum leverage ratio. We were in compliance with all financial co Credit Agreement at December 31, 2021.

## 7. Income Taxes

## 9. Incentive Plans

The Insperity, Inc. 2012 Incentive Plan, as amended, provides for options and other stock-based awards that have been and may b employees and non-employee directors of Insperity or its subsidiaries. The 2012 Incentive Plan permits stock options, including non and options intended to qualify as 'incentive stock options' within the meaning of Section 422 of the Internal Revenue Code, stock a awards, stock appreciation rights, performance units, and other stock-based awards and cash awards, all of which may or may not achievement of one or more performance objectives. The purpose of the 2012 Incentive Plan generally is to retain and attract perso experience and ability to serve as employees of Insperity and its subsidiaries and to serve as non-employee directors of Insperity, t proprietorship of such persons and to stimulate the active interest of such persons in the development and financial success of Insp

The 2012 Incentive Plan is administered by the Compensation Committee of the Board (the 'Committee'). The Committee has the p eligible employees will receive awards, the timing and manner of the grant of such awards, the exercise price of stock options (whic market value on the date of grant), the number of shares and all of the terms of the awards. The Board may at any time amend or te Incentive Plan. However, no amendment that would impair the rights of any participant, with respect to outstanding grants, can be m participant's prior consent. Stockholder approval of amendments to the 2012 Incentive Plan is necessary only when required by app exchange rules. Assuming all outstanding awards are paid at max, at December 31, 2021, 1,511,140 shares of common stock were grants under the 2012 Incentive Plan.

We also maintain the Insperity, Inc. Long-Term Incentive Plan ('LTIP') under the 2012 Incentive Plan. The LTIP provides for perform compensation awards in the form of performance units to certain employees based on the achievement of pre-established performa performance units under the LTIP to our named executive officers and certain other officers in 2019, 2020 and 2021.

Beginning with stock-based awards granted in 2020, employees who attain a minimum age of 62 and have provided 15 years or mo may continue to vest in awards following a qualifying retirement as defined under the 2012 Incentive Plan award agreement, as tho an employee, provided the grant date of the award is six months or more before the employee's last day of employment. For a termi qualifying retirement, time-vested awards will continue to vest in the normal course. For a termination following a qualifying retireme awards with completed or in-process performance periods are adjusted for achievement of the performance criteria, prorated throug and paid in the normal course, while performance-based awards for performance periods that have not started are forfeited. Stockexpense related to time-vested and performance-based awards is accelerated over the requisite service period for employees who for continued vesting.

## 10. Net Income Per Share

We utilize the two-class method to compute net income per share. The two-class method allocates a portion of net income to partici includes unvested awards of share-based payments with non-forfeitable rights to receive dividends. Net income allocated to unvest payments is excluded from net income allocated to common shares. Any undistributed losses resulting from dividends exceeding n allocated to participating securities. Basic net income per share is computed by dividing net income allocated to common shares by number of common shares outstanding during the period. Diluted net income per share is computed by dividing net income allocate the weighted average number of common shares outstanding during the period, plus the dilutive effect of time-vested and performa outstanding stock options.

## Securities Class Action Lawsuit

In July 2020, a federal securities class action was filed against us and certain of our officers in the United States District Court for th New York. The name of the case is Building Trades Pension Fund of Western Pennsylvania v. Insperity, Inc. et al. , Case No. 1:20-c October 23, 2020, the court issued an order appointing Oakland County Employees' Retirement System and Oakland County Voluntary Employees' Beneficiary Association Tru Plaintiff'). On December 22, 2020, the Lead Plaintiff filed its consolidated complaint alleging that we made materially false and misle regarding our business and operations in violation of the federal securities laws and seeking unspecified damages, attorneys' fees, injunctive relief, and such other relief that may be deemed proper. On April 26, 2021, the defendants moved to dismiss the consolid prejudice. The Lead Plaintiff filed its opposition to the motion to dismiss on June 10, 2021, and the defendants filed their reply in sup dismiss on July 12, 2021. We believe the allegations in the action are without merit, and we intend to vigorously defend this litigatio uncertainty regarding the outcome of this matter, no provision has been made in the accompanying Consolidated Financial Stateme

## Description of Business

## Revenue and Direct Cost Recognition

## Description of Business

Insperity, Inc. ('Insperity' or 'we', 'our', and 'us') provides an array of human resources ('HR') and business solutions designed to hel performance. Since our formation in 1986, we have evolved from being solely a professional employer organization ('PEO'), an indust current position as a comprehensive business performance solutions provider. We were organized as a corporation in 1986 and have since inception.

Our most comprehensive HR services offerings are provided through our Workforce Optimization ® and Workforce Synchronization TM s 'PEO HR Outsourcing solutions'), which encompass a broad range of human resources functions, including payroll and employment a benefits, workers' compensation, government compliance, performance management and training and development services, along wi human capital management platform, Insperity Premier TM .

In addition to our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions, we offer a comprehensive traditional payroll and human capital management solutio Acceleration. We also offer a number of other business performance solutions, including Performance Management, Organizational Pl Services, Employment Screening, Retirement Services and Insurance Services, many of which are offered as a cloud-based software products or services are offered separately or with our other solutions.

We provide our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions by entering into a co-employment relationship with our clients, under which Insperity a responsibility for certain portions of the employer-employee relationship. Insperity and its clients designate each party's responsibilities Service Agreement ('CSA'), under which Insperity becomes an employer of the employees who work at the client's location ('WSEE') and regulatory purposes.

As a co-employer of our WSEEs, we assume many of the rights and obligations associated with being an employer. We enter into an e with each WSEE, thereby maintaining a variety of employer rights, including the right to hire or terminate employees, the right to evalu qualifications or performance, and the right to establish employee compensation levels. Typically, Insperity only exercises these rights i clients or when necessary to ensure regulatory compliance. The responsibilities associated with our role as employer include the follo regard to our WSEEs: (1) to compensate our WSEEs through wages and salaries; (2) to pay the employer portion of payroll-related ta remit (where applicable) the employee portion of payroll-related taxes; (4) to provide employee benefit programs; and (5) to provide wo insurance coverage.

In addition to our assumption of employer status for our WSEEs, our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions also include other human resourc clients to support the effective and efficient use of personnel in their business operations. To provide these functions, we maintain a sig professionals trained in a wide variety of human resources functions, including employee training, employee recruiting, employee perfo employee compensation and employer liability management. These professionals interact and consult with clients on a daily basis to h service requirements and to ensure that we are providing appropriate and timely human capital management services.

## Revenue and Direct Cost Recognition

We enter into contracts with our customers for human resources services based on a stated rate and price in the contract. Our contrac pricing for a period of 12 months, and are generally cancellable at any time by either party with 30-days' notice. Our performance oblig services are rendered each month. The term between invoicing and when our performance obligations are satisfied is not significant. typically due concurrently with the invoicing of our PEO services. We do not have significant financing components or significant paym

Our revenue is generally recognized ratably over the payroll period as WSEEs perform their service at the client worksite in accordanc Standards Update ('ASU') No. 2014-09, Revenue from Contracts with Customers (Topic 606) . Customers are invoiced concurrently wi of its WSEEs. Revenues that have been recognized but not invoiced represent unbilled accounts receivable included in accounts receivable, net on our Consolidated Balance Sheets.

Pursuant to the 'practical expedients' provided under ASU No 2014-09, we expense sales commissions when incurred because the te cancellable by either party with a 30-day notice. These costs are recorded in commissions in our Consolidated Statements of Operatio

Our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions revenues are primarily derived from our gross billings, which are based on (1) the payroll cost of o markup computed as a percentage of the payroll cost. The gross billings are invoiced concurrently with each periodic payroll of our W exclude the payroll cost component of gross billings and therefore consist solely of markup, are recognized ratably over the payroll per their service at the client worksite.

In determining the pricing of the markup component of our gross billings, we take into consideration our estimates of the costs directly WSEEs, including payroll taxes, benefits and workers' compensation costs, plus an acceptable gross profit margin. As a result, our op significantly impacted by our ability to accurately estimate, control and manage our direct costs relative to the revenues derived from th our gross billings.

Consistent with our revenue recognition policy, our direct costs do not include the payroll cost of our WSEEs. Our direct costs associat generating activities are primarily comprised of all other costs related to our WSEEs, such as the employer portion of payroll-related ta plan premiums and workers' compensation insurance costs.

## Leases

We determine if an arrangement is a lease at inception of a contract in accordance with ASU No. 2016-02, Leases (Topic 842) as well Accounting Standards Board issued ASUs clarifying the lease guidance. ROU assets represent our right to use an underlying asset for lease liabilities represent our obligation to make lease payments arising from the lease. ROU assets and lease liabilities are recognize commencement date based on the present value of lease payments over the lease term. As most of our leases do not provide an impli our incremental borrowing rate based on the information available at commencement date in determining the present value of lease pa terms used to calculate the ROU asset and related lease liability include options to extend or terminate the lease when it is reasonably exercise that option. Lease expense for operating leases is recognized on a straight-line basis over the lease term as an operating exp agreements which require payments for lease and non-lease components and have elected to account for these as a single lease com other operating facilities. Please read Note 11, 'Leases,' for additional information.

## Workers' Compensation Costs

We provide group health insurance coverage to our WSEEs in our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions through a national network of carrie UnitedHealthcare ('United'), UnitedHealthcare of California, Kaiser Permanente, Blue Shield of California, HMSA BlueCross BlueShiel of which provide fully insured policies or service contracts.

Approximately 87% of our costs related to health insurance coverage are provided under our policy with United. While the policy with plan, as a result of certain contractual terms, we have accounted for this plan since its inception using a partially self-funded insurance Effective January 1, 2020, under the amended agreement with United, we no longer have financial responsibilities for participant's ann exceed $1 million. Accordingly, we record the cost of the United plan, including an estimate of the incurred claims, taxes and administr the 'Plan Costs') as benefits expense, which is a component of direct costs, in our Consolidated Statements of Operations. The estim based upon: (1) the level of claims processed during each quarter; (2) estimated completion rates based upon recent claim developme plan; and (3) the number of participants in the plan, including both active and COBRA enrollees. Each reporting period, changes in the costs resulting from claim trends, plan design and migration, participant demographics and other factors are incorporated into the bene requires a significant level of judgment.

Additionally, since the plan's inception, under the terms of the contract, United establishes cash funding rates 90 days in advance of th reporting quarter. If the Plan Costs for a reporting quarter are greater than the premiums paid and owed to United, a deficit in the plan liability for the excess costs would be accrued in our Consolidated Balance Sheets. On the other hand, if the Plan Costs for the reporti the premiums paid and owed to United, a surplus in the plan would be incurred and we would record an asset for the excess premium in our Consolidated Balance Sheets. The terms of the arrangement require us to maintain an accumulated cash surplus in th which is reported as long-term prepaid insurance. In addition, United requires a deposit equal to approximately one day of c which was $6.5 million as of December 31, 2021, and is reported as a long-term asset. As of December 31, 2021, Plan Cost net premiums paid and owed to United by $22.0 million. As this amount is less than the agreed-upon $9.0 million surplus $31.0 million difference is included in accrued health insurance costs, a current liability, in our Consolidated Balance Sheet including the additional quarterly premiums, owed to United at December 31, 2021, were $12.6 million, which is also inclu insurance costs, a current liability in our Consolidated Balance Sheets. ## Workers' Compensation Costs

Our workers' compensation coverage for our WSEEs in our PEO HR Outsourcing solutions has been provided through an arrangemen of Insurance Companies or its predecessors (the 'Chubb Program') since 2007. The Chubb Program is fully insured in that Chubb has all claims incurred under the policy regardless of whether we satisfy our responsibilities. Under the Chubb Program, for claims incurred September 30, 2019, we have financial responsibility to Chubb for the first $1 million layer of claims per occurrence and, for claims ove maximum aggregate amount of $6 million per policy year for claims that exceed $1 million. Chubb bears the financial responsibility for these levels. Effective for claims incurred on or after October 1, 2019, we have financial responsibility to Chubb for the first $1.5 million occurrence and, for claims over $1.5 million, up to a maximum aggregate amount of $6 million per policy year for claims that exceed $

Because we bear the financial responsibility for claims up to the levels noted above, such claims, which are the primary component of compensation costs, are recorded in the period incurred. Workers' compensation insurance includes ongoing health care and indemnit claims are paid over numerous years following the date of injury. Accordingly, the accrual of related incurred costs in each reporting pe which take into account the ongoing development of claims and therefore requires a significant level of judgment.

We utilize a third-party actuary to estimate our loss development rate, which is primarily based upon the nature of WSEEs' job respons WSEEs, the historical frequency and severity of workers' compensation claims, and an estimate of future cost trends. Each reporting p actuarial assumptions resulting from changes in actual claims experience and other trends are incorporated into our workers' compens estimates. During the years ended December 31, 2021, 2020 and 2019, we reduced accrued workers' compensation costs by $41.7 m $31.7 million, respectively, for changes in estimated losses related to prior reporting periods. Workers' compensation cost estimates ar value at a rate based upon the U.S. Treasury rates that correspond with the weighted average estimated claim payout period (the aver 0.6% in both 2021 and 2020) and are accreted over the estimated claim payment period and included as a component of direct costs i Statements of Operations.

The current portion of accrued workers' compensation costs on the Consolidated Balance Sheets at December 31, 2021 and 2020 incl $2.7 million, respectively, of workers' compensation administrative fees.

The undiscounted accrued workers' compensation costs were $255.1 million as of December 31, 2021 and $257.9 million as of Dece

At the beginning of each policy period, the workers' compensation insurance carrier establishes monthly funding requirements compris and funds to be set aside for payment of future claims ('claim funds'). The level of claim funds is primarily based upon anticipated WS expected workers' compensation loss rates, as determined by the insurance carrier. Monies funded into the program for incurred claim within one year are recorded as restricted cash, a short-term asset, while the remainder of claim funds are included in deposits, a long Consolidated Balance Sheets. In 2021, we received $35.1 million for the return of excess claim funds related to the workers' compens decreased deposits. As of December 31, 2021, we had restricted cash of $46.9 million and deposits of $185.0 million.

Our estimate of incurred claim costs expected to be paid within one year is included in current liabilities, while our estimate of incurred be paid beyond one year is included in noncurrent liabilities on our Consolidated Balance Sheets.

## Stock-Based Compensation

At December 31, 2021, we have one stock-based employee compensation plan under which we may issue awards. We account for thi recognition and measurement principles of ASC 718, Compensation - Stock Compensation , which requires all share-based payments grants of employee stock options, to be recognized in the income statement based on their fair values.

We generally make annual grants of unrestricted stock under our stock-based incentive compensation plan to our non-employee direct management but changed to restricted stock units for employees in 2020. Restricted stock and restricted stock unit grants to officers a generally vest over a period of three years from the date of grant. Shares of restricted stock and restricted stock units are valued base date of grant and the associated expense, net of estimated forfeitures, is recognized over the requisite service period. Stock grants iss directors are 100% vested on the grant date.

During 2020, we also granted performance-based restricted stock units to officers and other management, which vest or expire based various performance metrics. Performance-based restricted stock units have a vesting schedule of one year. The fair value of each pe restricted stock unit is the market price of our common stock on the date of grant. The compensation expense for such awards is reco basis over the requisite service period. Over the performance period, the number of shares expected to be issued is adjusted upward the probability of achievement of the performance target.

Our Insperity Long-Term Incentive Program (the 'LTIP') provides for performance based long-term compensation awards in the form o certain employees based on the achievement of pre-established performance goals. Each performance unit represents the right to rec at a future date based on our performance against certain targets. Performance units have a vesting schedule of three years. A portion employees was considered a market-based performance award that cliff vests at the end of three years assuming continued employm market-based performance goals. The fair value of each performance unit is the market price of our common stock on the date of gran market-based performance unit was determined through use of the Monte Carlo simulation method. The compensation expense for su on a straight line basis over the vesting term. Over the performance period the number of shares expected to be issued is adjusted up on the probability of achievement of the performance target.

## Company-Sponsored 401(k) Retirement Plans

Under our 401(k) retirement plan for corporate employees (the 'Corporate Plan'), we matched 100% of eligible corporate employees' c from 3% to 6% of the employees' eligible compensation in 2021 and up to 6% of the employees' eligible compensation in 2020 and 20 contributions under the Corporate Plan are immediately vested. During 2021, 2020 and 2019, we made matching contributions on beh employees to the Corporate Plan of $8.2 million, $12.6 million and $11.4 million, respectively, and is included in salaries, wages and p Consolidated Statements of Operations.

Advertising

Income Taxes

Under our separate 401(k) retirement plan for WSEEs (the 'Worksite Employee Plan'), the match percentage for WSEEs ranges from 0% to 6%, as determined by each client company. Matching contributions under the Worksite Employee Plan a During 2021, 2020 and 2019, we made matching contributions on behalf of WSEEs to the Worksite Employee Plan of $244 million and $198.2 million, respectively.

Advertising

We expense all advertising costs as incurred.

## Income Taxes

We use the liability method in accounting for income taxes. Under this method, deferred tax assets and liabilities are determined base financial reporting and income tax carrying amounts of assets and liabilities and are measured using the enacted tax rates and laws in differences are expected to reverse. Please read Note 7, 'Income Taxes,' for additional information.
